- **`TruthState`** — the simulation's ground-truth analogue, the same kinematic/dynamic fields without covariance/validity. The truth-vs-onboard separation (§2.3) is therefore a **type distinction**: the FSW is structurally unable to consume `TruthState`.
- **Versioned:** both structs carry a schema version (§22.1) so serialized logs, telemetry, and golden fixtures remain readable as the state evolves.
- **Derived views, not copies:** the multi-frame representations in telemetry (ECI, ECEF, Keplerian, geodetic, LVLH/RIC — §20) are **computed views** of the one `EstimatedState`, so they cannot disagree with each other or with what the controller used.

### 8.1 Attitude Determination
- **Fine mode (nominal):** **MEKF (multiplicative EKF)** — unit-quaternion reference (JPL scalar-first) + 3-parameter error state, gyro-bias states; star tracker(s), multi-IMU, and multi-sun-sensor fusion feed measurements.
  - **Consistent with the truth side, and singularity-free.** The filter's estimate *is* a unit quaternion, propagated by the same kinematics the truth plant uses (§5.1) — so both sides carry one global attitude representation with no singularity. The **3-parameter error state is not a second representation**: a 3-DOF rotation cannot carry a full-rank 4×4 covariance (the unit-norm constraint makes it singular), so the covariance is defined on a minimal local rotation error (GRP/rotation-vector) that is multiplicatively composed onto the quaternion and reset to zero each update. That is the entire content of "multiplicative" EKF — the quaternion never leaves SO(3), and the error parameterization is a linearization detail local to one step, not a mode with its own singularity.
- **Coarse mode (degraded / safe):** when star trackers are unavailable (occlusion, fault, slew-limited), estimation falls back to **sun sensor + magnetometer vector measurements + IMU gyro propagation**. The two body-frame vectors (sun direction, magnetic field) are paired with their inertial references (sun ephemeris, onboard IGRF-14 modeled field, §5.2/§6.2) to form a coarse attitude; the IMU propagates attitude between/through vector updates. This is the estimator used in **Safe** and **Sun Point** acquisition and whenever the fine solution is invalid.
- **Initialization / coarse determination:** deterministic single-frame initializers (**TRIAD / QUEST / q-method**) seed both the coarse solution and the MEKF from two vector measurements, giving a defined cold-start/acquisition path rather than assuming a pre-converged filter.
- **Mode arbitration:** transitions between fine and coarse are driven by sensor validity (§9.1) and surfaced to FDIR and the mode manager (§10); the active estimation mode is telemetered.
- Consistency monitoring (NEES/NIS) as diagnostics.

**Coarse attitude chain (Implemented Push 39), `lib/` side.** Phase 4 opens with the estimator the Safe-mode floor rests on: **TRIAD + coarse SS+MAG+IMU**, in the flight-safe `lib/gnc` (fixed-size Eigen, no heap, no exceptions, return codes checked, finiteness-guarded output; no F´ types and no I/O, so the F´ `AttitudeEstimator` component of the next push is a thin wrapper). `gnc::triad` (`lib/gnc/triad.{hpp,cpp}`) is the deterministic single-frame initializer (Black 1964 [black1964]; Markley & Crassidis §5.2 [markley2014]): two body/inertial vector pairs in, `Quat<Body, ECI>` out, with the **sun pair as primary** (TRIAD fits the primary exactly, so the better-known direction leads) and the magnetic pair resolving the roll about it. It also returns **Shuster's covariance of the TRIAD solution** ([shuster1981], under the QUEST measurement model `E[δbᵢδbᵢᵀ] = σᵢ²(I − b̂ᵢb̂ᵢᵀ)`), expressed on the body-frame error `δθ` so it seeds the MEKF directly; the `1/sin²θ` growth of the roll variance *is* the geometry telling you the solution is going unobservable. Near-parallel pairs — a normal flight condition, the sun and the field lines do line up — come back as an **invalid solution**, never an assert, and the geometry gate is applied in **both** frames so measurements that disagree with the models by more than the geometry are rejected rather than published. `gnc::CoarseAttitudeEstimator` (`lib/gnc/coarse_attitude.{hpp,cpp}`) is the estimator proper: closed-form quaternion kinematics on the bias-corrected gyro between updates, a TRIAD solve whenever both pairs are fresh/valid/well-conditioned, and a **fixed-gain complementary blend** along the eigenaxis of the propagated-to-TRIAD error rotation — deliberately not a Kalman update, because the coarse mode's job is a few-degree *always-available* answer and a filter that can diverge is the wrong thing to put under the safe-mode floor (the MEKF is where optimal fusion belongs). The covariance is propagated (`Φ P Φᵀ` + gyro angle random walk) and blended with the same gain (`(1−k)²P⁻ + k²R`), **with a systematic floor**: blending alone would drive the reported uncertainty towards `k/(2−k)` of one TRIAD fix, which is a lie for a coarse budget, because most of it — analytic-ephemeris error, IGRF model error, sensor alignment — is the *same offset every cycle* and does not average down no matter how many fixes you take. Each source's uncertainty is therefore configured as a **white** part and a **systematic** part; TRIAD is solved on the white part (its covariance is the reducible one), the systematic part is re-evaluated on the same geometry through the exposed `gnc::triadCovariance` (the Shuster covariance is linear in the two variances, so the two evaluations sum to the full one-shot uncertainty), and the published covariance is the sum. It therefore converges to the systematic floor rather than to zero, which is what makes it safe to hand to the MEKF. The covariance is re-symmetrised after every propagation and update so downstream Cholesky factorisations cannot fail on accumulated asymmetry. **Eclipse and dropout are the designed-for cases:** no sun vector means gyro-only coasting with a growing covariance and a reported age; past the configured coast horizon the attitude is declared **invalid** rather than left to drift quietly, and the solution is dropped so the next TRIAD **re-acquires whole** instead of blending against a prior that no longer means anything. A gap longer than the configured max step is treated as a dropout (attitude held) rather than extrapolated on a stale rate; a **non-increasing** clock is refused without destroying the solution — backwards is obvious, but a *stuck* clock is the dangerous one, since re-running the update at the same epoch would fold the same measurement in twice and shrink the covariance on information already used; non-finite measurements are excluded per §9.1 rather than used. A refused cycle returns a default-constructed product, so nothing half-written or stale is ever published behind a cleared validity flag. The product is written into the canonical `EstimatedState` (§8.0) by `gnc::writeToEstimatedState` — attitude, rate, their validity, the attitude block of the 15×15 covariance (written only when the attitude is valid, so an invalid solution cannot stamp a meaningless covariance over the last good one), and mode `Coarse`/`Invalid` — leaving the orbit fields to §8.3. Verified by `tests/unit/triad_test.cpp` and `tests/unit/coarse_attitude_test.cpp` (25 tests): exact recovery of random attitudes; the covariance checked analytically at orthogonal geometry, as a full 3×3 in the `{b̂₁, m̂, n̂}` triad basis at 60° separation (which is what pins the off-diagonal coupling term), for linearity across independent error budgets, and by 20 000-sample Monte Carlo whitened against the reported covariance (sample NEES = 3); degenerate-geometry and malformed-input rejection; exact propagation through a 30 s eclipse; coast-horizon invalidation and whole re-acquisition; blend convergence bounded *below* by the systematic floor and above by one raw fix; stuck- and backwards-clock refusal; and `q0 ≥ 0` and covariance positive-definiteness maintained throughout. Satisfies REQ-ADET-002 and REQ-ADET-003.

**`AttitudeEstimator` component (Implemented Push 40), flight side.** The Push 39 chain now runs on the vehicle. `flight.AttitudeEstimator` (`flight/PolarisFsw/AttitudeEstimator/`) is a passive F´ component on the **barrier-driven 10 Hz GNC cycle** (§2.4): it is member 0 of that rate group, so estimation happens before anything that would act on the estimate. Per cycle it selects the first valid, fresh unit of each sensor type off its measurement port arrays, assembles the two inertial references, runs one `CoarseAttitudeEstimator::update`, and publishes the §8.0 product on `estimateOut` for the guidance/control consumers of later pushes. No estimation math and no I/O live in the component — the algorithm stays in `lib/gnc`, the field model in `lib/environment`, the tables behind the `OnboardTables` ports; the `EstimatedState` mapping is `gnc::writeToEstimatedState`, so the component cannot invent its own idea of what "valid" or "Coarse" means.

**Multi-unit is the port contract from the start.** The measurement inputs (`imuIn`, `sunSensorIn`, `magnetometerIn`, `gnssIn`, `starTrackerIn`) are **port arrays** sized `GncMaxUnits = 8`, defined once in the interface-only `GncPorts` module (`ImuMeas`, `SunSensorMeas`, `MagnetometerMeas`, `GnssMeas`, `StarTrackerMeas`, `AttitudeEstimate`) that producer and consumer share. The vehicle will fly several sun sensors, magnetometers and IMUs and one or more star trackers, and the §8.2 fusion layer and the MEKF must not have to rework a port interface to get them: adding a unit is a topology line plus a vehicle-config entry. This push consumes the **first valid unit** of each type — a deterministic priority in vehicle build order (§19.4), explicitly *not* fusion — and the star-tracker input is declared and its arrivals counted but **never stored or fused**, because the coarse mode has to stay tracker-independent to remain the §10 Safe-mode floor. Under SITL the measurements come from `SitlBridge`, which now decodes the per-unit sensor records the STEP_REQ has always carried and republishes them on that seam **before** cycling the rate group, so the FSW sees this step's measurements in the cycle the barrier drives; a request whose length does not match the HELLO-declared suite is rejected whole, leaving the previous step's measurements intact rather than half-overwritten. On hardware the same ports are filled by `Drv` sensor drivers and the SITL subtopology is absent — which is the reason the seam is a shared port module rather than a SITL type.

**The references, and what they cost.** The **sun reference** is `OnboardTables.getBodyPosition(SUN)`, normalised and made spacecraft-centric when a position is known, carrying the query's **source grade** through to telemetry; because the ephemeris falls back to the analytic Sun (Push 38), it answers with or without an uploaded table, which is what keeps the safe-mode floor table-independent. The **magnetic reference** is the onboard **IGRF-14** evaluated at the current position and rotated ECEF→ECI with onboard EOP. Two consequences are stated rather than hidden. First, the FSW needs an IGRF snapshot onboard, so the IAGA coefficient parser moved into the flight-safe `lib/environment/igrf_iaga.{hpp,cpp}` (`<cstdio>`, fixed buffers, no `std::string`/`std::vector`, load-time only) and `sim/world/igrf_file.cpp` became a `std::string` façade over it — one parser, no drift; the topology loads the snapshot at setup (`-I`, default the committed verbatim IAGA file) and a failure is an `IgrfLoadFailed` warning, after which the estimator still publishes body rate (Safe-mode rate damping needs it) but can never acquire attitude. Second, **position comes from the §8.3 orbit estimator** (Push 65; it came straight off GNSS until then): the `OrbitEstimator` component runs first in the GNC rate group and publishes its ECI solution once per cycle on `orbitStateIn`, and the attitude estimator **consumes it once** — the latch is cleared on read, so a producer that stops running leaves position unavailable rather than a stale vector wearing a valid flag. The receiver never reaches this component; the §9.1 plausibility band on a fix is the orbit filter's gate now, and what remains here is the gate a port cannot promise, finiteness — because an unchecked position does not fail loudly downstream: it poisons *both* references (a non-finite radius through the field model, a non-finite sun direction through the spacecraft-centric correction) while every validity flag still reads true. A receiver outage therefore costs the magnetic pair only once the orbit filter has dropped its solution at its coast horizon; when it has, the estimator gyro-coasts, flagged by `PositionUnavailable`. The IGRF snapshot carries its own horizon too: the loader records the year past which its linear model stops being the published one (the next tabulated IAGA epoch, or five years past the last), and the estimator refuses the magnetic reference beyond it rather than extrapolating — anchoring that horizon to the *base* epoch instead would expire a snapshot taken late in a grid interval within weeks of launch. That is surfaced as an edge-gated `PositionUnavailable` warning rather than papered over with a guessed position — the interim is honest and its removal is §8.3's job. The reference grade telemetered each cycle is the **worse** of the ephemeris and EOP grades, with edge-gated `ReferenceDegraded`/`ReferenceRecovered` events following the Push 38 `TableDegraded` pattern.

**Tuning has no defaults, and refusal is the failure mode.** All ten tuning values (the white/systematic sun and magnetic sigmas, gyro ARW, TRIAD geometry gate and blend gain, coast and step horizons, and the measurement staleness gate; the geocentric-radius band moved to the orbit estimator with the fix it gates, Push 65) are **F´ parameters with no defaults**, read from `ParameterDb` (§19.3). A missing or out-of-range value emits an edge-gated `ConfigInvalid` warning and leaves the estimator inert with mode `INVALID` telemetered — it never substitutes a value, because a coarse estimator running on an invented noise budget reports a covariance that is fiction, and the whole point of this estimator is a covariance trustworthy enough to seed the MEKF. That was exactly what a default SITL run showed until Push 41 wired the config-compiler→`ParameterDb` path (§19.3): `ConfigInvalid` once, mode `INVALID`, no attitude. The reference vehicle's tuning now ships in `config/spacecraft/leo_smallsat.yaml` and is derived from the units it actually carries — the GomSpace NanoSense FSS field-edge accuracy, its Earth-albedo error and the analytic-ephemeris floor for the sun pair, the generic magnetometer's noise against a ~30 µT LEO field and its uncalibrated hard-iron bias for the magnetic pair, the STIM300 angle random walk for the gyro — so the covariance the estimator reports is the one its own hardware justifies. A stuck or backwards master clock is refused and counted in the component rather than looking like a quiet no-op cycle; `RESET_ESTIMATOR` drops the solution, re-arms the alerts and re-reads the tuning. Health telemetry covers mode, quaternion, body rate, covariance trace, solution age, TRIAD accept/reject counts, refused cycles, reference grade, per-source validity (gyro/sun/mag/position) and star-tracker count; `AttitudeAcquired` and `AttitudeLost` mark the acquisition and coast-expiry edges for FDIR. **Partially satisfies REQ-ADET-004** as of Push 40: the active mode is telemetered and its acquisition/loss edges are surfaced as events, which is the clause this push closes. Two clauses stayed open — the NEES/NIS consistency diagnostic, which belongs to the MEKF because a fixed-gain complementary blend has no innovation sequence to compute it from, and fine↔coarse transition handling, which needs a fine mode to transition to — and were closed by Push 42 and Push 44 respectively. Verified by the deployment's first **F´ component test harness** (`flight/PolarisFsw/AttitudeEstimator/test/ut/`, 6 tests): acquisition of a known attitude from synthetic sun/magnetometer/gyro measurements through the *real* reference assembly — with the blend gain at 1 so any frame error in that assembly shows up directly in the published quaternion — eclipse coast to expiry with a single `AttitudeLost` and whole re-acquisition, refusal without parameters, the staleness gate excluding a perfectly consistent but stale measurement set, grade passthrough with one degrade and one recover alert, and position loss blocking the magnetic pair while the body rate keeps flowing. The estimation math itself stays pinned at the `lib/gnc` level (Push 39), so the harness tests only what the component adds. Alongside it, `tests/unit/sitl_handler_test.cpp` covers the sensor-record decode (positional unit identity, rejected-message atomicity) and `tests/unit/igrf_test.cpp` the flight IGRF loader against the sim façade coefficient for coefficient, while the two-process `tests/integration/sim_sitl_lockstep_test.cpp` runs the real binary with the estimator wired into the barrier cycle. What that particular test pins is narrower than it may look: it spawns the deployment with no parameter file, so the estimator refuses every cycle and what is demonstrated is that an inert new rate-group member leaves the truth trace bit-identical — the measurement publication path and the cycle plumbing execute, the estimation does not. The closed-loop assertion is `tests/integration/sitl_attitude_tuning_test.cpp` (Push 41), which runs the whole chain — `leo_smallsat.yaml` → `configc` → `PrmDb.dat` → `prmDb` → `attitudeEstimator` — and reads the deployment's own event stream back: every declared parameter loaded, no `ConfigInvalid`, and `AttitudeAcquired` on the first cycle with a sun/magnetometer pair. Wiring it exposed that the deployment's hand-rolled `setupTopology` had never called the generated `readParameters()` phase at all, so `prmDb` had been loading nothing regardless of what file it was pointed at. Writing that first harness surfaced a repo-wide defect worth recording: `cmake/Dependencies.cmake` was forcing `BUILD_TESTING` off to suppress Eigen's test tree, and since F´ keys `register_fprime_ut` off the same flag, every F´ component unit test in the deployment would have been silently skipped; the flag is now saved and restored across the fetch.

**Fine mode — Davenport q-method + MEKF (Implemented Push 42), `lib/` side.** The optimal-fusion half of §8.1, in the same flight-safe `lib/gnc` (fixed-size Eigen, no heap, no exceptions, bounded loops, finiteness-guarded output; the F´ component wiring is a later push).

`gnc::davenport` (`lib/gnc/davenport.{hpp,cpp}`) generalises the acquisition path from TRIAD's exactly-two pairs to **N** weighted ones, solving **Wahba's problem** by Davenport's q-method (Davenport 1968 [davenport1968]; Markley & Crassidis §5.3 [markley2014]): the weighted attitude profile `B = Σ wᵢ b̂ᵢ r̂ᵢᵀ` becomes the symmetric 4×4 `K = [[σ, zᵀ], [z, S − σI]]` — written **scalar-first** to match the JPL layout (§3.3), which is Markley's K with the scalar row/column permuted, no convention translation needed — whose largest-eigenvalue eigenvector *is* the optimal quaternion. It is solved with Eigen's fixed-size `SelfAdjointEigenSolver<Matrix4d>` rather than QUEST's characteristic-polynomial Newton iteration: on a 4×4 the microseconds QUEST saves are worth far less than a bounded iteration budget with an explicit `info()` status when that budget is exhausted, and QUEST's documented failure mode when the top two eigenvalues approach each other has no error return at all. Weights are the maximum-likelihood `wᵢ = 1/σᵢ²`, which is what makes the reported covariance the **inverse Fisher information** `P = [Σ σᵢ⁻²(I − b̂ᵢb̂ᵢᵀ)]⁻¹` (Shuster & Oh 1981 [shuster1981]) — Shuster's QUEST covariance, which belongs to the Wahba *optimum* rather than to the algorithm that finds it, so it applies to the q-method verbatim. The Wahba loss at the optimum is returned as a diagnostic: it is the residual no rotation can explain, so it catches observations that disagree with *each other* (a mis-modelled reference, a mis-wired sensor) — a fault signature geometry gating cannot see. Observability is gated as `λ_min/λ_max` of the information matrix, scale-free so it gates geometry rather than noise level, applied in **both** frames for the same reason TRIAD is; collinear sets, malformed inputs and non-convergence are refusals, never asserts.

`gnc::Mekf` (`lib/gnc/mekf.{hpp,cpp}`) is the fine estimator: a 6-state multiplicative EKF (Lefferts, Markley & Shuster 1982 [lefferts1982]; Markley & Crassidis §6.2.4 [markley2014]) over `x = [δθ; δb]` — body-frame attitude error and gyro-bias error, in the **same block order as `state::ErrorState`**, so the canonical 15×15 covariance takes its blocks verbatim. The reference attitude stays a unit quaternion propagated by the same closed-form constant-rate kinematics the coarse estimator and the truth plant use; the error state is composed multiplicatively and reset to zero every update, so the quaternion never leaves SO(3) and the 3-parameter error is a linearisation detail rather than a second representation. `δθ` is defined by `q_true = δq ⊗ q̂` — the opposite sign to the convention TRIAD's covariance is quoted in, which is exactly why a TRIAD or Davenport solution seeds the filter directly: a covariance is unchanged by `δθ → −δθ`.

Two pieces of the propagation are done in full rather than approximated, and both are load-bearing for **bias observability**. The state transition carries the exact `Φ₁₂ = −∫₀^Δt exp(−[ω̂×]s) ds` in closed form (with `Φ₁₁` taken as the propagation increment's own attitude matrix, so the two cannot disagree), and the discrete process noise keeps its cross-coupling blocks — `Qd = [[(σ_v²Δt + σ_u²Δt³/3)I, −½σ_u²Δt²I], [−½σ_u²Δt²I, σ_u²Δt I]]` (Farrenkopf 1978 [farrenkopf1978]; the Markley Eq. 6.93 form), the van Loan integral of this `F` and `Q_c`. The `Δt²` term is the statement that an attitude error and a bias error of the right sign are the *same* error seen twice; a diagonal shortcut discards that correlation. This `Qd` is the one place the propagation is approximate: it neglects rotation of the noise axes over a step, i.e. it assumes `|ω|Δt ≪ 1`, which at the 10 Hz GNC rate and any rate a controlled vehicle sees is exact to well below the noise itself. `Φ` is not approximated. On a step where the gyro was **not** used (dead, non-finite, or a gap past `max_dt_s`) `Φ₁₂` is **zero** rather than `−Δt·I`: the bias estimate took no part in a propagation that did not happen, so it caused none of that step's attitude error, and carrying the coupling anyway would invent a correlation the first measurement afterwards would "correct" by dragging the bias.

Vector measurements are folded **one at a time** — sun, then field, then any further pair — so the §8.2 N-sensor fusion layer is more calls rather than an interface change or a growing matrix. Each is a unit direction with `H = [[b̂_pred×] 0₃]`, a **Joseph-form** covariance update (symmetric positive-definite under round-off and under a gain that is not exactly optimal — which is the case the moment `R` is inflated), and an exact axis-angle multiplicative reset so a large re-acquisition correction stays a proper rotation. Measurement noise is a **per-update, caller-supplied σ** giving `R = σ²I`: isotropic rather than the rank-2 QUEST form because the transverse form makes `S = HPHᵀ + R` singular along `b̂` while the along-vector innovation carries no attitude information anyway. The header states the consequence rather than hiding it — **the filter treats `R` as white**, so fusing a systematic budget (analytic-ephemeris bias, IGRF model error, alignment: the terms the coarse estimator splits out as a covariance floor) without inflating σ makes the filter overconfident, averaging down an offset that does not average down. The filter cannot tell the two apart and does not pretend to; the caller inflates σ.

**Consistency is a product, not a test artefact (REQ-ADET-004).** Every update returns its innovation, `S`, and the **NIS** `yᵀS⁻¹y`, and a measurement past the configured `nis_gate` is **rejected and counted** — the divergence guard, since an outlier folded in at full gain drags the reference attitude somewhere the filter never comes back from. The gate is a χ² threshold on **2** degrees of freedom, not 3: the innovation between two unit vectors is transverse by construction, so the third component `R = σ²I` budgets for carries no variance. One rejection is a χ² tail; a *stream* of them is what FDIR watches the count for. `Mekf::nees` computes the 6-state NEES against a known truth (Bar-Shalom §5.4 [barshalom2001]) for analysis and Monte-Carlo validation — there is no truth onboard, so it has no flight use.

Refusal follows the coarse mode's rules — unconfigured, uninitialised, non-increasing epoch (backwards *and* stuck), non-finite or zero-length inputs, non-positive σ: all refuse without touching the solution, and a non-finite internal result drops the filter to cold start *without* clearing the rejection count, since a filter that has just been dropped is when FDIR most needs to see what it had been rejecting on the way there (only a commanded reset clears it). `initialize` is treated as a trust boundary and rejects a seed covariance that is not **positive-definite**, not merely finite: an indefinite P makes S indefinite, and the NIS could then come back negative — which is why the gate is written as an accept range `0 ≤ NIS ≤ gate` rather than a one-sided "too large?" test that a negative value sails straight through. Silent acceptance is the one failure mode a divergence guard must not have. Tuning has **no defaults** (`arw_rad_per_sqrt_s`, `rrw_rad_per_s_per_sqrt_s`, `nis_gate`, `max_coast_s`, `max_dt_s`), gated by `MekfConfig::isValid` at construction. One behaviour deliberately differs from the coarse mode: past `max_coast_s` the attitude reads invalid but the solution is **not** dropped, because a Kalman gain against a grown covariance already takes the returning measurement almost whole — there is nothing to gain by throwing away a converged bias estimate, which is the opposite of the coarse blend's fixed gain. Per the project decision the deterministic single-frame solvers are **cold-start/re-init only** and never run in the steady-state loop. The product goes into the canonical `EstimatedState` via the overloaded `gnc::writeToEstimatedState`: attitude, rate, gyro bias, their validity, the attitude and gyro-bias covariance blocks **including their cross terms** (a consumer reasoning about the two together needs the correlation), and mode `Fine`/`Invalid` — orbit fields left to §8.3, and the blocks written only when the attitude is valid.

Verified by `tests/unit/davenport_test.cpp` and `tests/unit/mekf_test.cpp` (19 tests). For the q-method: exact recovery of random attitudes from 2–5 pairs, the closed-form `P = (σ²/2)I` at three orthogonal equal-weight observations, monotone tightening with an added observation, a 20 000-sample Monte Carlo whitened against the reported covariance (sample NEES = 3), a mean-square win over TRIAD on the same noisy two-vector data (which is what a wrong weighting would invert), loss detection on a reversed observation, and collinear/malformed refusal. For the MEKF: exact propagation on a perfect gyro over a 30 s tumble, gyro-bias convergence to within 10% of an injected 0.06 °/s truth, cold-start convergence from a *noisy* Davenport seed with the covariance shrinking honestly alongside it, and the headline **150-run Monte Carlo** with every stochastic input drawn from the distribution the filter assumes — mean NEES **6.01** against 6 (99% interval ±0.73) and mean NIS **2.02** against 2 over 54 000 updates. Plus outlier rejection leaving attitude, covariance and age bit-unchanged while the next good measurement is still accepted; dead-gyro, non-finite-gyro and overlong-gap all holding the attitude and growing only the noise; stuck- and backwards-clock refusal proven by **bit-identity** against a filter that never saw the faulted cycles; malformed-measurement refusal distinguished from gate rejection in the counters, a non-positive-definite seed covariance refused at `initialize`, and a **direct algebraic check** of one propagation step against a hand-computed `ΦPΦᵀ + Qd`. That last one exists because no statistical test can see Qd's cross blocks — at any realistic tuning they sit six orders of magnitude below `Q11` — so zeroing or sign-flipping them would otherwise pass every other test in the suite; it also pins `Φ₁₂ = 0` on a step where the gyro was not used. Satisfies REQ-ADET-001 and closes the NEES/NIS clause of REQ-ADET-004; the fine↔coarse arbitration clause stayed open until the component wiring of Push 44 below.

**Fine mode on the vehicle — MEKF wired onto `AttitudeEstimator` with arbitration (Implemented Push 44), flight side.** The Push 42 filter now runs onboard, and the mode arbitration §8.1 asks for is a real state machine rather than a telemetry field with one possible value. `flight.AttitudeEstimator` gained a second estimator, a second validity gate on its tuning, and the promotion/demotion logic between them.

**The coarse chain runs every cycle, whatever the mode.** That is the structural decision the rest follows from: the fallback under a demotion is a *live* coarse solution, not one that has to re-acquire from cold at the moment the vehicle has just lost confidence in its filter. Cost is one TRIAD solve per 10 Hz frame, plus — in the eligible-but-unpromoted state, where the preconditions hold every cycle but the solve keeps failing — one Davenport 4×4 eigendecomposition per frame on top of it; that state is bounded by geometry that resolves within an orbit, and both are what the safe-mode floor is worth. **Promotion**: once the coarse attitude is valid and both vector pairs are present in the same cycle, a `gnc::davenport` solve over those pairs seeds the MEKF — attitude and covariance straight from the solve, bias zero at the configured turn-on sigma — and `FineModeEngaged` reports the seed's covariance trace. The engaging cycle still **publishes coarse**: `Mekf::initialize` seeds an attitude but no rate, since nothing has been propagated yet, so publishing the filter immediately would inject a one-cycle `rateValid == false` and a zero body rate into the GNC product while the coarse chain had a perfectly good rate in hand. The filter takes over on the next cycle, which also makes promotion symmetric with the one-cycle rule demotion already follows. Demotion, by contrast, takes effect **immediately** — falling back is never worth delaying. Davenport rather than TRIAD even at N = 2, because the seed is the one place the weighting matters and the q-method is the optimum TRIAD approximates; it is also the interface the §8.2 fusion layer will hand more observations to without changing anything here. The coarse validity is not needed by the solve — it is required anyway, as the cheap statement that the vehicle has a working floor before it starts trusting a filter on top of it. **Demotion** is `FineModeDemoted` with a reason FDIR can act on differently: `REFUSAL_STREAK` (the filter refused propagate/update on N consecutive cycles — malformed input, a software fault), `NIS_STREAK` (the gate rejected a measurement on N consecutive cycles — a measurement stream the filter no longer believes), `COAST` (nothing accepted inside the fine coast horizon), `FILTER_FAULT` (a non-finite internal result dropped the filter itself), `COMMANDED` (`RESET_ESTIMATOR`, or a tuning change that rebuilt the filter). Telling a *gate rejection* apart from a *refusal* is done on `Mekf::rejectedCount`, not on the return value, because both return false and they mean opposite things — one is the divergence guard working, the other is the filter refusing input it cannot use — and conflating them would make both streaks meaningless. A demotion **drops the filter state**: a solution no longer trusted is not worth resuming, so re-promotion runs a fresh Davenport seed. Demotion and promotion are at least one cycle apart, so a condition that oscillates appears in the event stream instead of hiding inside a single cycle. And because the coarse solution is live underneath, a demotion emits **no** `AttitudeLost` — the acquisition/loss edges are keyed on the *published* solution, so they mean what a consumer gating on the validity flag sees, and a mode change that loses nothing does not look like a loss. What a mode change *does* discontinue is the published body rate: coarse publishes the raw gyro, fine publishes it bias-corrected, so promotion and demotion step the rate by the estimated bias (a few 0.01 °/s on this hardware). That is the honest value in each mode rather than a transient, and a rate consumer that cannot take a step of that size at a telemetered mode transition should be filtering, not the estimator.

Dropping the filter would also drop `Mekf::rejectedCount`, which `Mekf::reset` clears by contract — a *commanded* reset being the operator saying "start over". That contract is exactly wrong for a demotion: `NIS_STREAK` and `FILTER_FAULT` would erase the FDIR signal at the moment they created it. The component therefore keeps its own running total across demotions, clears it only on `RESET_ESTIMATOR`, and telemeters both it (`MekfRejectedTotal`, the one to trend) and the live filter's count.

**Two validity gates on the tuning, and the granularity is the point.** Seven new no-default parameters (`MekfRrw`, `MekfNisGate`, `MekfMaxCoastSec`, `MekfBiasSigmaInit`, `MekfRefusalStreak`, `MekfNisStreak`, `SeedMinObservability`) come through the same Push 41 config-compiler→`ParameterDb` path, taking the reference vehicle to nineteen. They are validated **separately** from the twelve coarse values: a missing coarse parameter leaves the vehicle with no attitude at all and keeps the fatal `ConfigInvalid`, while a missing fine parameter emits `FineConfigInvalid` and leaves the component running coarse-only — a flyable vehicle on the §10 floor, which is not a state to refuse the whole estimator over. One cross-field gate ties the two sets together: `MekfMaxCoastSec ≤ MaxCoastSec`. The live-fallback premise requires the coarse chain to outlast the fine one, and a fine mode configured to coast longer would demote onto a fallback that had already gone invalid — the single case where a demotion really does lose the attitude. The MEKF's angle random walk and largest propagation step are deliberately **not** new parameters: they are the coarse chain's `GyroArw` and `MaxDtSec`, the same gyro and the same rate group, and two parameters for one physical quantity is two chances to disagree. The per-update sigma handed to the filter is each source's white and systematic parts **root-sum-squared**, derived from the coarse budget so the two estimators cannot be told different things about the same sensor. That inflation is required by `Mekf`'s white-`R` model, and it is worth naming its limit: with only sun and magnetometer as vector sources the systematic terms dominate the budget, so the filter's reported covariance is optimistic against the true systematic floor even inflated — the honest fix is a measurement source whose error genuinely is white, which is the star tracker the §8.2 layer brings in. The reference vehicle's values are derived in `config/spacecraft/leo_smallsat.yaml` from the units it carries: σ_u from the STIM300's 0.3 °/hr Allan bias-instability floor through the Gauss-Markov relation (≈0.437 σ at the floor) and the modelled 100 s correlation time, the NIS gate as χ²₂ at 99.9% = 13.82, the initial bias sigma as the STIM300's 10 °/hr turn-on repeatability, the seed observability gate as `sin²(θ/2) = 0.0076` at the same 10° separation the coarse chain's `MinSinAngle` gates (the information-matrix eigenvalue ratio for two equally-weighted observations is `(1 − cos θ)/2`, *not* `sin²θ/2` — the two agree only at 90°, which is where a sanity check would sit, and the wrong form gates at ~14°), and a **300 s** fine coast horizon against the coarse 2400 s — the two answer different questions, and eclipse alone does not reach it because the magnetic pair still updates the filter in shadow; it takes losing *both* sources, which is a fault.

New health telemetry: `MekfNis` (the **largest** NIS of this cycle's updates, accepted or rejected — the onboard half of the consistency diagnostic; the maximum rather than the last, because a rejection sits above the gate and an accepted update below it, so reporting the last would let a good magnetic update hide the sun outlier that preceded it), `MekfRejected`, `MekfRejectedTotal`, `GyroBias`, `FineAttCovTrace`, `BiasCovTrace`, `FineDemotions`. Verified by six new tests in the component harness (`flight/PolarisFsw/AttitudeEstimator/test/ut/`, 15 total): promotion off a Davenport seed with an injected 0.06 °/s gyro bias converging over 60 s and the fine covariance falling below a third of the seed's — the whole reason to run a filter, and the thing the coarse chain structurally cannot do, since it estimates no bias at all — with the engaging cycle pinned to still publish a valid coarse rate; demotion on an NIS rejection streak driven by a sun vector 60° from where the filter says it must be, with the magnetic pair kept honest so the streak is specifically a *rejection* streak, and the running rejection total surviving the demotion that zeroes the filter's own; demotion on a *refusal* streak driven by a zero-length but finite sun vector, which clears the component's finiteness gate and is then refused as unnormalisable rather than gate-rejected — leaving the rejection total at zero, since a refusal is not an outlier and counting it as one would have FDIR chasing the wrong fault; demotion on the fine coast horizon with both sources withdrawn, inside the coarse horizon so the published attitude survives; **no demotion producing an `AttitudeLost`**; coarse-only operation with one `FineConfigInvalid` when the fine tuning is absent; and `RESET_ESTIMATOR` demoting with reason `COMMANDED` and re-seeding. `FILTER_FAULT` is the one demotion path left unexercised in the harness: it needs a non-finite *internal* result, and every route to one from outside passes a finiteness gate in the component or in `Mekf::update` first, so pinning it would test the harness rather than the component — the filter's own fault handling is pinned in `tests/unit/mekf_test.cpp`. On the vehicle, `tests/integration/sitl_attitude_tuning_test.cpp` now also asserts nineteen parameter records loaded, no refusal from either gate, `FineModeEngaged`, and **no demotion** over the run — a demotion inside a 10 s run with good geometry and healthy sensors would mean the flight tuning is wrong. The covariance comparison stays in the component harness rather than moving there: the only channel back from the deployment in that test is its text event stream, and a periodic covariance EVR added to serve a test would be telemetry noise on a real vehicle. **Closes the fine↔coarse arbitration clause of REQ-ADET-004**, and with it the requirement: mode telemetered (Push 40), NEES/NIS diagnostic (Push 42), transitions driven by sensor validity and surfaced to FDIR (this push).

**Attitude-knowledge accuracy: the metric and what the reference suite actually delivers (Push 45).** Knowledge accuracy is stated on the **error norm** — the total eigenaxis angle between the estimated and the true attitude, `θ_err = 2·arccos|q_err,0|` (evaluated as `2·atan2(‖q_err,v‖, |q_err,0|)` per §3.3), quoted at the 3σ (99.73rd-percentile) point of a Monte Carlo campaign. The norm and not a per-axis split: it is rotation-invariant, so it holds whatever body-axis convention the vehicle adopts, and it is the quantity a pointing budget consumes directly. A boresight/cross-boresight split is not the vehicle-level form: axis-resolved knowledge belongs to the **per-sensor cross-boresight requirements** a payload brings with it (+Z the sensor boresight), while the vehicle-level knowledge requirements stay total-norm. The three thresholds are **REQ-ADET-005** (coarse, ≤ 5°), **REQ-ADET-006** (fine on sun + magnetometer, ≤ 3° with the DE440 ephemeris tables active) and **REQ-ADET-007** (fine with two star trackers fused, ≤ 0.03° as enacted in Push 52, down from 0.05°), all under the geometry condition that the sun and field directions are at least 45° apart, and all verified — the first two now, the third when §8.2 lands — by `tests/unit/attitude_accuracy_mc_test.cpp`, an 800-run campaign on the reference vehicle's own budget whose sample maximum is a distribution-free 88%-confidence upper bound on the 99.73rd percentile.

Two of those numbers are worth stating plainly, because they are the *measured* answer rather than the hoped-for one. **First, the coarse and fine thresholds are equal.** The systematic-floor argument above says a constant offset does not average down; the campaign quantifies it. With a 2.04° 1σ per-axis systematic on the sun pair and 1.93° on the magnetic pair, the coarse chain measures a median error norm of 3.3° and a 3σ bound of 8.5°, and the MEKF on the same two sources measures 2.9° and 8.1° — better on both, but by 0.4° on a floor of 8°, which is why one threshold covers both. The filter is not merely tied, though, and the campaign is built to settle that question honestly: **both chains are driven by the same draw** — geometry, truth motion, systematic biases and every noise sequence — so coarse-vs-fine is a *paired* comparison rather than two samples of the same budget. Paired, the MEKF is the more accurate of the two on **65% of the 800 runs**, with a median per-run improvement of 0.20°; under "equally good" the win count would be Binomial(800, ½) = 50 ± 1.8%, so that is not sampling noise, and the win fraction holds between 64.2% and 65.4% across every master seed tried. What the filter cannot buy is a *different floor*: with only two vector sources neither a fixed-gain blend nor an optimal filter can tell a constant offset from the truth, and the tail of both is the Rayleigh tail of the systematic draw (3.44σ = 7.0° for the sun pair alone; the worst runs are the ones that drew a large sun *and* a large magnetic offset together, with no NIS rejections and no coasting involved). Improving this is a **sensor-budget** action — hard-iron calibration, albedo correction, a tracker — not an estimator-tuning action; what the filter does buy beyond the 0.4° is gyro-bias observability and a covariance that means something, neither of which this metric can see. **Second, the geometry condition is not a dodge.** The solution's component about the sun is fixed by the magnetic pair alone and its error grows as 1/sin(separation); over the full band the flight gate admits (down to sin 10°) the same campaign measures ~19° in both modes, driven entirely by runs below ~30° of separation. That is the observation geometry, not the estimator, and below the gate the designed behaviour is to refuse the solve and coast (REQ-ADET-002). **Third, on the norm metric a single star tracker is dominated by its about-boresight axis**: the AURIGA's Z terms RSS to ≈ 95 arcsec 3σ against ≈ 16 arcsec cross-boresight, plus the unit's fixed 61 arcsec bias that does not average down at all, so ≈ 115 arcsec looked like the hardware alone — which is why REQ-ADET-007 was *written* at 0.05° rather than the ~0.02° a cross-boresight-only reading of the datasheet suggests. **Push 52 measured both halves of that sentence and corrected one.** The estimate was pessimistic: it added a 3σ noise RSS to the bias *bound* as if they were the same kind of quantity, where the bound is on an isotropic offset whose per-axis 1σ is `B/3` = 20.4 arcsec, and the measured single-tracker figure is 86 arcsec. The mode also changed, by user decision: **it does not keep the SS+MAG suite** — with a tracker fused the vector pairs are demoted to FDIR-monitored residuals rather than folded in (§8.2). Two trackers with **non-parallel** boresights, each covering the other's weak axis, do buy the 0.02°-class figure — measured 0.021°, and better than one tracker on 65% of paired runs — but by less than a noise-only reading of the datasheet suggests, because the floor they share is the king tracker's own bias and nothing removes that.

**Calibration roadmap and the committed post-calibration thresholds (user decision, 2026-08-02).** The current thresholds are honest to the *uncalibrated* reference suite; they are not the end state. Three capability items are committed, each attacking one named term of the budget above: **(1) onboard magnetometer hard/soft-iron calibration** — an ellipsoid fit of the raw field magnitude against the onboard IGRF over a tumble's worth of data, flight-standard, recovering the 1.9° hard-iron term to the ~0.2–0.5° class; **(2) albedo correction in the sun-vector reconstruction** — *implemented, Push 47*: a centroid albedo model against the §5 geometry (`lib/gnc/albedo_correction`, applied at the one point in `AttitudeEstimator` ahead of every sun consumer), recovering the deterministic part of the 2.0° albedo term and leaving its dispersion — 2.04° → 0.72° on the reference vehicle; **(3) a second star tracker with a non-parallel boresight** (§8.2 fusion), covering the first tracker's about-boresight weak axis. When (1) and (2) land, **REQ-ADET-005 tightens to 5° and REQ-ADET-006 to 3°**; when (3) lands, **REQ-ADET-007 tightens to 0.02°** — these exact figures, not approximations, are the committed post-calibration thresholds, and each tightening is enacted by re-running the same Monte Carlo campaign against the improved budget and moving the requirement value with the evidence, the same way the current values were set. The calibration items slot with Phase 4's remaining work (§8.2/§8.3) and the §24 phase list carries them.

**Calibration is commanded, executed, and assessed on orbit (user decision, 2026-08-02).** These are flight features, not ground-processing conveniences. The magnetometer calibration is the first push after the requirements land and is deliberately first because it is **attitude-free**: the hard/soft-iron ellipsoid fit needs only raw field samples spanning orientations against the onboard IGRF field *magnitude* — no attitude solution enters the fit, so it works from Safe mode on an uncalibrated vehicle. Command flow: `MAG_CAL_START` → a bounded collection window (tumble or commanded scan) → onboard ellipsoid fit → offset vector + scale/soft-iron matrix applied in the magnetometer processing path → fit residual and quality telemetered → correction persisted through the §19.3 parameter path. A SITL scenario demonstrates the full loop — command, collect, fit, apply — and the accuracy campaign re-run with the post-calibration budget is the evidence that moves REQ-ADET-005/006 toward their committed values. The sun-sensor side splits in two: the **albedo correction is a model, not a commanded calibration** (it needs geometry, not collected data) and landed independently in Push 47; the **commanded SS bias/alignment calibration is sequenced after §8.2 tracker fusion**, because it needs an attitude reference better than the sun sensor itself (§8.2).

Being a model rather than a command shapes the whole of its flight design. It is **always on** wherever the geometry supports it — no window, no fitted state, nothing to persist across a reboot — and its three parameters (the unit's peak albedo error, its field of view, its body-frame boresight) are pure configuration through the §19.3 path, validated as a **fourth independent gate** alongside the coarse, fine and calibration sets. A missing albedo parameter therefore costs neither the estimator nor a mode: it emits one edge-gated `AlbedoConfigInvalid` and the vehicle flies exactly as it did before the correction existed. What it *does* need every cycle is a position fix, the ECEF→ECI rotation, a sunlit Earth in the sensor's field, and an attitude to place that field with — the last being the previous cycle's published solution. When any of those is missing the correction is **skipped rather than guessed**: correcting on invented geometry would inject a bias the size of the one being removed, pointed in an arbitrary direction, which is strictly worse than not correcting.

**How much the attitude error costs is worth getting right, because the obvious answer is wrong by about six times.** The tempting reading is that an attitude error ε perturbs the pull *magnitude* φ by a relative ε and is therefore negligible. It is not: the dominant term is the rotation **axis**. `â = (ŝ × d̂)/‖ŝ × d̂‖` swings by `ε/sin ψ`, so the error in the correction vector is `φ·(ε/sin ψ) = A·Φ·η·ε` — set by the **peak** scale A (12° for the reference part) and *not* by the applied φ, present at full size even where the correction itself is small. The `sin ψ` cancellation is exactly what hides it from the naive estimate. Measured at **≈0.1° of sun-vector error per degree of attitude error** (0.22°/deg worst case). That is a function of how well the attitude is known *this cycle*, not a constant, so it is carried **dynamically**: the per-cycle *albedo* term is `SigmaSunAlbedoRad ⊕ A·σ_att/2`, with σ_att read from the published covariance trace, which then composes with the ephemeris term. At a converged fine-mode solution (~0.6°) the term is ~1 mrad and invisible in the static budget; right after acquisition, a slew or a re-acquisition it dominates, and those are precisely the cycles where gating on attitude *validity* alone — which cannot tell a 0.5° solution from a 10° one — would have reported the converged number and been overconfident in the unsafe direction.

That refusal is why the sun budget carries **two** albedo parameters, `SigmaSunAlbedoRad` (correction ran) and `SigmaSunAlbedoUncorrRad`, selected per cycle and telemetered through `SunAlbedoCorrection` (NaN meaning "did not run", so the ground can see which was in force). A single value would be wrong on one class of cycle or the other, and the coarse chain's covariance is the whole reason it exists — it seeds the MEKF — so it is told which kind of cycle this was rather than left to assume. The component refuses a pair with the uncorrected value tighter than the corrected one: a correction that made the measurement worse is a configuration error, not a flight condition.

**The sun systematic is composed per cycle from two independent terms (Push 48).** Once the albedo correction had removed most of the sensor term, the *reference* term — how well the vehicle knows where the Sun is — was over half of what remained: 7.0 mrad of analytic-ephemeris error (§11.3) against a 10.5 mrad albedo residual. But the analytic fallback is not what the vehicle has to fly on. The onboard DE440 Chebyshev tables (§11.3, uploaded and activated through `OnboardTables`) give an **arcsecond-class sun direction from time alone** — no position needed, and the ~10 arcsec LEO parallax is something the estimator already subtracts when it has a fix. So the composition is

```
sigma_sun_sys = hypot(albedo term, ephemeris term)
```

with the albedo term chosen by whether the correction ran (plus the runtime attitude-quality inflation above) and the **ephemeris term chosen by the grade the query answered at** — `SigmaSunEphemPreciseRad` while the tables cover the epoch, `SigmaSunEphemRad` on the fallback. That grading already existed: the per-domain `TableGrade` the Push 40 reference assembly tracks, with its `ReferenceDegraded`/`ReferenceRecovered` alerts. Push 48 is the plumbing that makes the *budget* follow it, on the same cycle, rather than flying one number for both cases.

Four parameters where there had been two, and they are kept apart because they fail and are fixed independently: a better sun sensor moves the albedo pair and nothing else; an ephemeris upload moves *nothing*, because the vehicle already carries both grades and simply starts using the tighter one. On the reference vehicle they compose to 10.5 mrad (corrected, tables), 12.6 mrad (corrected, fallback), 34.9 mrad (uncorrected, tables) and 35.6 mrad (uncorrected, fallback). `SigmaSunEphemPreciseRad` is deliberately a parameter rather than a hardcoded zero — what an uploaded table is worth is the ground's statement about its own upload, and "we assumed zero" is not a thing to discover in flight. No new telemetry channel: `OnboardTables.EphemGrade` already publishes the served grade and the estimator's own EPHEMERIS-domain alerts mark every transition, so which term was in force is reconstructible from what is already downlinked. Carrying the boresight as a parameter rather than on the measurement port is deliberate too — mounting is configuration, not measurement, and putting a constant on a 10 Hz wire record would be the wrong seam. It also made `Vec3F64` the first **array-typed** F´ parameter, which the config compiler now encodes (a boresight split into three scalar parameters is three chances for them to disagree about which vector they describe).

**The magnetometer fit, and what it measures (Push 46, `lib/gnc/mag_calibration`).** The library half of that command flow is implemented and measured. Eliminating the unknown attitude from `m = S·B + b` against the onboard IGRF magnitude `|B| = F` leaves the ellipsoid `(m−b)ᵀA(m−b) = F²`, `A = S⁻ᵀS⁻¹`, which is **linear in ten parameters** (`A`, `A·b`, `bᵀAb`) — Alonso & Shuster's complete centered model [alonso2002complete], read as an ellipsoid after [vasconcelos2011]. Because the right-hand side carries the IGRF magnitude it is *inhomogeneous*, so there is no scale ambiguity to normalise away, and the fit is one batch linear least-squares solve rather than an iteration. The normal equations accumulate **sample by sample**: the accumulator is O(1) in the window length, so the component streams a collection window through it and never stores it. TWOSTEP's maximum-likelihood treatment [alonso2002twostep] exists to remove the second-order noise bias of exactly this estimator, and is deliberately not used — at 0.05 µT on a 30 µT field that bias is parts in 10⁶, three orders below the accuracy target, and it would buy an unbounded convergence loop on a flight path. Magnitudes constrain `S⁻¹` only up to a left rotation; the **symmetric positive-definite square root** `A^{1/2}` [higham2008] is the resolution, correct here because a permeability tensor is symmetric and any residual mounting rotation belongs to the alignment calibration, not this one.
Every unobservable case is a **refusal**: too few samples (algebraic minimum 10, reference config 100), an ill-conditioned normal matrix, a non-positive-definite fitted quadric, and a fit whose residual fails to beat the uncalibrated one by a configured factor — a calibration that makes the sensor worse is the outcome guarded hardest against. Orientation diversity has its own gate on a **coverage metric** `3·λ_min(D)`, `D` the second-moment matrix of the sampled field directions: unit trace makes `1/3` the isotropic eigenvalue, so the metric reads 1.0 for isotropic or hemispherical coverage and 0 for directions confined to a plane, with the closed form `(3/2)·⟨sin²θ⟩` over a cone of half-angle α giving 0.09 at 20° and 0.40 at 45°. It is exposed **during** collection, not only at the solve, because it is the number that tells the ground the vehicle has tumbled far enough. **Measured:** on the reference budget (1 µT hard iron, 5 mrad soft iron, 0.05 µT sensor noise, 100 nT IGRF magnitude error and a 0.5% IGRF scale error, 200 samples), the fit leaves a **0.087° worst-case and 0.058° median** residual systematic over 32 seeds — comfortably inside the 0.2–0.5° class this item was committed at, and a ~22× reduction of the 1.93° term.
**What that buys, and what it does not (informative campaign case).** Re-running the same 800-run campaign with only the magnetic systematic replaced by 0.087° moves the **medians** a long way — coarse 3.35° → 2.62°, fine 2.95° → **1.61°** — and the **3σ bounds** hardly at all: coarse 8.49° → 7.82°, fine 8.07° → 7.76°. That is not a disappointment, it is the systematic-floor argument reappearing: with the magnetic term gone the tail is the **sun** budget alone, whose 2.04° per-axis systematic has a 3.44σ Rayleigh tail at ~7.0°. So item (1) on its own does **not** reach the committed 5°/3°, and the committed tightening correctly names items (1) *and* (2) together — **the albedo correction is the binding one for the tail**, and this measurement is the evidence for sequencing it next. The projection is printed by an explicitly non-verifying case in `tests/unit/attitude_accuracy_mc_test.cpp`; REQ-ADET-005/006 stayed at 15° through this push, and were enacted at 5°/3° only once item (2) and the ephemeris grading had landed too.

**Both corrections together, and where that leaves the committed thresholds (Push 47).** With item (2) implemented the same 800 runs can be re-flown with *both* substitutions — the magnetic systematic at the post-calibration residual and the sun systematic at the post-albedo-correction one — and this is the measurement the §8.1 tightening decision rests on. The post-correction sun budget is derived the same way the uncalibrated one was: the correction removes the deterministic albedo term exactly and leaves the truth model's dispersion, so 0.30 × 34.9 mrad = 10.5 mrad of albedo residual against the untouched 7.0 mrad analytic-ephemeris term, RSS 12.6 mrad — a **2.04° → 0.72°** per-axis sun systematic. Measured: **coarse median 3.35° → 1.01°, 3σ bound 8.49° → 2.77°; fine median 2.95° → 0.60°, 3σ bound 8.07° → 2.69°**. One modelling caveat travels with those numbers: the campaign injects the post-correction residual as a **constant per-run systematic**, the same way it treats every other systematic term, whereas the real residual is proportional to the albedo pull and therefore varies over an orbit with the field-fill fraction and the dayside factor. The per-run constant is the conservative reading — it never lets the term average down — but it is a coarser model than the one the correction itself uses, and a geometry-proportional treatment is what a campaign flown against the truth models rather than against the budget would give. Both clear the committed 5°/3°, coarse with 45% margin and fine with 10%. Two things about that result are worth stating rather than celebrating. **First, the fine margin is thin**, and it is thin against a threshold the sun budget now dominates on its own; a vehicle over ground more variable than the model assumes would eat it. **Second, and more importantly, the number turns on the dispersion fraction, not on the algorithm.** The correction removes what it models to round-off; everything the vehicle gains is decided by how well a uniform Lambertian sphere stands in for the real Earth, and 0.30 is an honest mid-range figure for a correction carrying no reflectivity data, not a measured property of this orbit. Read the projection as a statement about that fraction. The two obvious ways to widen the fine margin follow from the same budget line: an onboard reflectivity model (Bhanderi & Bak's TOMS-driven treatment [bhanderi2005]) attacks the dispersion, and the **analytic sun ephemeris** — untouched by all of this, and now over half the remaining sun budget at 7.0 mrad against 10.5 — attacks the other half.

**With the ephemeris tables active as well (Push 48), and the framing the tightening should use.** Re-running the same 800 runs with the sun systematic at its tables-active value (10.5 mrad rather than 12.6) measures **coarse median 0.85°, 3σ bound 2.48°; fine median 0.51°, 3σ bound 2.25°** — 50% and 25% margin against the committed 5°/3°, against 45% and 10% on the analytic fallback. The fine margin more than doubles, which is the point: the thin margin the albedo push reported was over half ephemeris error, and the vehicle already carries the fix.

**The tightening, enacted (Push 50).** With all three items — magnetometer calibration, albedo correction and ephemeris grading — landed and running on the flight chain, **REQ-ADET-005 is 5° and REQ-ADET-006 is 3°**, and the verifying campaign in `tests/unit/attitude_accuracy_mc_test.cpp` now runs against the **post-calibration budget** rather than the uncalibrated one. Measured against the enacted thresholds: coarse **2.48° against 5°, 50% margin**; fine **2.25° against 3°, 25% margin**. Both clear the 20% `margin_required`, and that margin is now **CI-enforced** — the campaign asserts the bound at or below 80% of each threshold, since `conf.py` has no `margin_achieved` gate and a margin nothing checks is a number, not a property.

**Both requirements are conditioned on the post-calibration parameter set**, and this is the honest statement of what the vehicle delivers when. The magnetometer fit tightens `SigmaMagSysRad` from 0.0337 to ~1.5e-3 and forces `SeedMinObservability` from 0.0076 to ~1.1e-4, and **neither value is what `config/spacecraft/leo_smallsat.yaml` ships** — they arrive by ground uplink after a successful on-orbit `MAG_CAL_START`. As delivered the vehicle therefore sits on the uncalibrated floor, **8.49° coarse and 8.07° fine**, recorded in both requirement bodies the same way the analytic-ephemeris floor is. That is a commissioning step rather than a defect: a hard-iron signature is a property of the integrated vehicle and cannot be known before flight, which is why §8.1 designs the calibration as commanded, executed and assessed on orbit. REQ-ADET-006 thus carries two conditions of unequal weight — the parameter set is the larger dependency (a campaign, a fit and an uplink, worth ~5.8° at the bound), the ephemeris tables the smaller (an upload, worth 0.44°, with the vehicle carrying both grades and selecting per cycle unaided). Losing the tables degrades; never having calibrated does not meet.

REQ-ADET-006 takes the **conditioned** form the previous push proposed: the 3° threshold is stated with the DE440 tables active, and the analytic fallback is recorded as a **degraded floor** rather than a violation — 2.69°, which clears 3° but at only ~10% margin, and it is that margin gap rather than the threshold that makes the conditioning necessary. REQ-ADET-005 is stated flat, because both cases clear 5° comfortably (2.48° and 2.77°, 50% and 45% margin) and a condition that never binds is a condition not worth carrying. The degraded floor keeps its own regression guard in the campaign (`PostBothCorrectionsFallbackIsTheDegradedFloor`), which asserts that losing the tables cannot beat having them and that the fallback still clears both thresholds — a regression past 3° there would change the conditioning story, not merely cost margin.

REQ-ADET-007 was still 0.05° and held at `reviewed` here: it needed the §8.2 fusion layer to be verifiable at all, and the ~0.02° figure waited on a second, non-parallel star tracker. **Both landed in Push 52** (§8.2 below), and the threshold is now **enacted at 0.03°**: measured **0.021°** on the reference vehicle's two-AURIGA suite, 30% margin. The committed 0.02° is deliberately *not* what was enacted — the measured bound sits just above it, so enacting it would have made the requirement fail — and 0.03° is the smallest value that evidence carries the declared 20% margin at. Like REQ-ADET-005/006 it is **conditioned**: on the post-calibration parameter set, and additionally on a valid inter-tracker alignment, with king-only recorded as the degraded floor at 0.024°.

One flight-parameter consequence surfaced while measuring this and now applies to the vehicle rather than only to the projection: the Davenport seed's observability gate `λ_min/λ_max` is scale-free under a *common* rescaling of the measurement σ's but **not** under changing them relative to each other, so once the magnetic pair is much tighter than the sun pair the shipped `fine.seedMinObservability = 0.0076` refuses every geometry in the band and must be re-derived wherever the calibration is enabled.

**That re-derivation, done properly (Push 51).** Splitting the information matrix `M = w_s(I − ŝŝᵀ) + w_m(I − m̂m̂ᵀ)` into its out-of-plane direction (eigenvalue `w_s + w_m`) and its 2×2 in-plane block (trace `w_s + w_m`, determinant `w_s w_m sin²θ`) gives the closed form `λ_min/λ_max = ½[1 − √(1 − 4 w_s w_m sin²θ/(w_s+w_m)²)]`, which collapses to `sin²(θ/2)` at equal weights — the shipped 0.0076 at θ = 10°. Post-fit the weights are not equal, and the value to uplink is **5.1e-4**, not the ~1.1e-4 this section previously carried. The earlier figure is the same formula evaluated with the **uncalibrated** sun budget (0.0356 systematic — the "~16× tighter magnetic pair" reading) against the *calibrated* magnetic one, and that is a parameter set the vehicle never flies: the albedo correction is a model, always on, needing no uplink, so any vehicle with a calibrated magnetometer also has the corrected sun budget. With both in force the sun total is `hypot(0.0116, 0.0126) = 0.01713` on the analytic ephemeris and `hypot(0.0116, 0.0105) = 0.01565` with the DE440 tables, against a magnetic total of `hypot(0.0017, 0.0015) = 0.00228`; the same 10° separation then reads **5.16e-4** (analytic) and **6.14e-4** (tables). 5.1e-4 is the number to uplink because the ephemeris grade changes per cycle without an uplink, so the gate must admit 10° in the worse of the two; being slightly loose in the other (it admits down to 9.1° there) errs toward accepting geometry the coarse chain's own `MinSinAngle = sin 10°` is still publishing at, which is the safe direction. The wrong 1.1e-4 would have gated at 4.6° — seeding the filter on geometry the coarse chain has already refused to solve. The derivation and both endpoints are recorded in the post-fit uplink block of `config/spacecraft/leo_smallsat.yaml`, next to the calibration tuning that triggers it.

**The commanded flow on the vehicle (Push 46 stage 2, `flight::AttitudeEstimator`) — implemented.** The library fit is now wrapped in the estimator component, so the whole of the flow above except the persistence step is flight code. `MAG_CAL_START(sampleCount)` opens a window, `MAG_CAL_ABORT` closes one without fitting, `MAG_CAL_CLEAR` drops an applied calibration, and `RESET_ESTIMATOR` does both as part of its full-reset semantics. The window is counted in **accepted samples, not seconds**, because a sample only enters when that cycle had both a valid magnetometer reading and a modelled IGRF field: a GNSS outage, a sensor dropout, or (once the §7 MTQ/MAG interlock supplies actuation state) a magnetorquer-on window all cost samples without costing wall-clock, so a duration would let the ground command a window that quietly collected a tenth of the data it asked for. **Collection does not disturb the running estimator** — it is a tap, the fit runs on **raw** samples so an applied calibration never refits itself, and the published attitude changes only when a fit is applied or cleared; the component test asserts bit-identical mode and attitude telemetry against a run with no window open. The correction is applied at **one point**, between magnetometer unit selection and every consumer, so the coarse chain, the MEKF and the future §8.5 B-dot law cannot disagree about what the field was; `applyMagCalibration` is called unconditionally and passes the raw vector through while nothing is applied, so an uncalibrated vehicle takes the same code path. The §7 interlock has its structural gate point there too, presently a constant `true` with no MTQ state reaching the component. Every refusal is telemetered with the gate that closed — `MagCalRejected(SAMPLES|COVERAGE|CONDITION|NO_IMPROVEMENT|NUMERICAL|CONFIG)` — because a refusal is only actionable if it says whether to collect longer, tumble further, or stop trying; nothing is applied on a refusal and a previously applied calibration is retained, since a failed later window is no information about the correction already flying. Health is `MagCalState` (IDLE/COLLECTING/APPLIED, derived not stored), `MagCalSamples`, `MagCalResidualAngle`, and **`MagCalCoverage` live during collection** — the last is the one that earns its place, because it tells the ground mid-window whether the vehicle has tumbled far enough, where a refusal at the end only says it had not. Seven new no-default parameters (`MagCalNominalFieldT`, `MagCal{Min,Max}FieldT`, `MagCalMinSamples`, `MagCalMinCoverage`, `MagCalMaxCondition`, `MagCalMinImprovement`) take the reference vehicle to **twenty-six**, past the F´ default `PRMDB_NUM_DB_ENTRIES` of 25 — raised to 64 by a `CONFIGURATION_OVERRIDES` module (`flight/config/`) rather than a vendored copy of the F´ config directory. They are a **third** validity gate and deliberately not a per-cycle one: they are read at `MAG_CAL_START` and a missing value refuses that *command* with `MagCalRejected(CONFIG)`, leaving the vehicle entirely unaffected, because calibration is a commanded activity rather than a flight function. Two structural details the component carries: `run` and all four commands are F´ **`guarded`** ports rather than `sync`, because the rate group and the command dispatcher are different threads and the handlers write exactly the state the estimation cycle reads — a torn read of the applied calibration is *finite in both halves*, so it would clear every validity gate and enter both estimator chains as a measurement, at the cost of one uncontended mutex per 10 Hz cycle; and a window **closes itself** after ten cycles per sample asked for, since a window counted in accepted samples is stalled rather than ended by a sensor outage, and the alternative is a vehicle telemetering COLLECTING forever. **Measured in SITL** (`tests/integration/sitl_attitude_tuning_test.cpp`): a 4500-sample window over a 450 s tumbling arc fits to a **2.2e-3 rad (0.13°)** residual at 0.45 coverage, against the 34 mrad uncalibrated systematic, with the estimator acquiring and holding fine mode throughout. **The constant is not free (Push 67).** The ten-parameter linear fit carries the quadric's constant `c` as its own unknown, but the model has none — `c = βᵀAβ` identically — so the solve returns `(m−b)ᵀA(m−b) = F² − κ` with `κ = c − βᵀAβ` decided by noise. On a real LEO arc `F` swings only ~6 % over a window, the constant column and `tr(A)` are nearly collinear, and κ was **not** small: on the reference vehicle it shipped as a constant 0.7 % magnitude scale error and read as a 7.45 mrad residual against a 2.2 mrad noise floor, invisible on the pre-Push-65 straight-line plant (which climbed ~1000 km and swept `F` far more) and in the unit tests (which sweep it ±35 %). The solve now absorbs κ into the scale of `A` — `A ← A·F̄²/(F̄²−κ)` — which removes it exactly at the mean field and leaves a term of order `κ·(F swing)` the residual then reports honestly; the fixed-`F` case is a unit test.

**Persistence and re-tuning are ground actions, by decision, not by omission.** The applied calibration lives in component state and **nothing is written to `ParameterDb`**: the ground reviews the quality telemetry and decides. Two consequences are documented rather than automated. First, the calibration **does not survive a reboot** — persisting it rides on the §23.6 non-volatile-state work, and until then a vehicle whose event log carries no `MagCalComplete` is running raw and the window must be re-flown. Second, a *good* fit invalidates two shipped parameters: `SigmaMagSysRad` describes an uncalibrated magnetometer (`SigmaMagWhiteRad` is unchanged — the fit removes the systematic, not the sensor noise), and `SeedMinObservability` was derived from the **ratio** of the sun and magnetic sigmas, so the 0.0076 above must be re-derived to **5.1e-4** with the calibration enabled (derivation above). The ops procedure — collect, watch coverage, grade the residual, uplink the three re-derived values, then reset and re-fly — is in `flight/PolarisFsw/README.md`. Auto-writing any of this would be a vehicle changing its own error budget without the ground having seen the evidence, which is exactly the failure mode §19.3 exists to prevent.

**The payload's share of that error, and what bounds it (Push 45).** The vehicle-level requirements stay on the total norm, and the axis-resolved form belongs to a payload. The metric is the **cross-boresight** error: the great-circle angle between where the payload's boresight actually points and where the attitude solution says it points, `∠(R(q̂)ᵀb, R(q)ᵀb)` for the mounted boresight `b` (sensor +Z, §6.3). It is the part of the knowledge error that displaces the scene on the focal plane; the remaining component is a rotation *about* the boresight, which turns the image about its own centre without moving where the instrument looks, and for a circularly symmetric instrument costs nothing at all. **REQ-PAY-001** bounds it at **10% of that sensor's smallest full field-of-view angle** (3σ) — `2 × half_fov` for a conic field, `2 × min(half_x, half_y)` for a rectangular one, the narrow axis being the one a pointing miss leaves the field through first; 0.8° for the 5°×4° generic imager. A fraction rather than an angle because the number an instrument cares about is what share of its own field a miss eats, and because it then scales with whatever payload the vehicle ends up carrying instead of needing a re-derivation each time. The requirement is evaluated in the mode a payload is actually operated in — **fine with star tracker(s) fused**, the mode of REQ-ADET-007 — and is verified per sensor by the §8.2 fusion push that makes that mode reachable, holding at `reviewed` until then exactly as REQ-ADET-007 does. Its first form was an absolute 14° taken from the SS+MAG+IMU campaign, which was the wrong anchor: it measured a mode no payload would image in, so the threshold was set by the magnetometer's hard-iron residual rather than by anything about the payload. With trackers fused the cross-boresight budget is ~16 arcsec-class per tracker, so 10% of any realistic field is comfortably feasible — the requirement guards a mis-specified suite or a mis-mounted instrument, not a stretch target. The Push-45 campaign still measures the projection on the SS+MAG+IMU mode it can reach, now as **informative evidence** rather than as verification: a median of 2.2° against 2.9° for the total norm on those same runs, a ratio of 0.74 matching the π/4 = 0.785 an isotropically directed error predicts, and — asserted, not assumed — the same answer on a deliberately canted mounting, since a mounting-dependent result would mean the campaign had a preferred body axis and would invalidate the vehicle-level numbers too.

**The NESC best practices on the fine filter (Push 71).** The MEKF as built is Markley's Ch. 8 of NASA/TP-2018-219822 [carpenter2018] — quaternion reference, three-parameter body-frame error, exact axis-angle reset, Farrenkopf `Q` with the cross terms — and it took the TP's Ch. 9 operability with the orbit filter (§8.3): `Mekf::retune` under a running solution (a fine-mode or *any* estimator parameter upload used to demote to coarse and lose the converged bias), `ATT_REINIT_COV` (re-open the covariance, keep attitude and bias — milder than `RESET_ESTIMATOR`, which loses the bias), the `Sun/Mag/StMeasMode` accept/inhibit/force policy (inhibit withholds the type from both chains; force applies past the fine gate and is counted in `MekfForced`), and the per-cycle LDLᵀ definiteness check (`FineCovarianceHealthy`). **Push 72 took Ch. 3, 5 and 8 the rest of the way (REQ-ADET-015):** the **order-invariant same-epoch update** (TP §3.2, Algorithm 3.1) — `Mekf::beginBatch`/`endBatch` around every epoch's trackers and pairs, each measurement linearised at the same reference with its innovation taken against the corrections already accumulated, one exact multiplicative reset per epoch (a propagate closes an open batch first, per §8.9.2), pinned by two orders agreeing to 1e-9° where sequential resets differ by 0.01° at a 5° prior error; the **Reynolds covariance reset** (Eq. 8.76 [reynolds2008]) on every reset, on by default (`MekfConfig::reynolds_reset`), the MC NEES campaign unchanged under it; a **first-order Gauss-Markov gyro bias** option (`MekfBiasTauSec`, §5.2.4: `Φ₂₂ = e^{−Δt/τ}`, `Q₂₂ = σ_u² τ/2 (1 − e^{−2Δt/τ})`, the estimate decaying with it) — **flown at 0** because the STIM300 model's turn-on bias is constant and a 100 s FOGM would decay it through a coast (§5.2.7), which the yaml records; and **star-tracker latency** (§3.1) modelled and compensated end to end: the truth tracker now has a delay line like the receiver's (`latency_s`, AURIGA 0.1 s = one frame, a modelling choice since no vendor quotes it), the sample keeps its exposure-epoch tag, `Mekf::updateAttitude(…, latency_s)` advances it on the filter's own bias-corrected rate (`q_now = exp([ω̂×]τ) ⊗ q_meas`) and inflates `R` by `σ_v² τ I + τ² P_bb`, and configc refuses a `MaxMeasAgeSec` that does not exceed the installed trackers' latency (the flight/sim pair). Measured: at the 0.5 °/s slew limit a 100 ms-latent tracker is 0.87 mrad — ten σ, gated — uncompensated, and 1e-6 rad compensated; on the component the slewing vehicle keeps its trackers fused with zero rejections and publishes the *current* attitude, and every star-tracker SITL row now flies the delayed tracker.

### 8.2 Sensor Fusion
- **Multi-IMU fusion — fault-tolerant by construction (user decision, 2026-08-02):** plain averaging is forbidden — its breakdown point is zero, so one railed unit reporting an unrealistic rate drags the mean unboundedly. The committed design, in order: **(1) per-unit plausibility gates** (rate magnitude against physical vehicle limits, finiteness, staleness) reject the obviously-railed reading before any combination; **(2) robust combination** — per-axis **median** for ≥ 3 units (tolerates one arbitrary fault by construction); with exactly 2 units, disagreement can be *detected* but not attributed, so the tie-break comes from a third information source (the MEKF's propagated-rate residual — the unit the filter's own dynamics disbelieve loses); **(3) exclusion is an FDIR event** — EVR, validity flag, exclusion latch with an explicit re-admission policy, wired into the §9 monitor architecture. Noise-optimal weighted fusion applies only to the surviving set: robustness before optimality.
- **Multi-sun-sensor fusion:** models output **diode counts**; the algorithm reconstructs a fused sun-direction vector with FOV/eclipse handling. The reference vehicle grows a realistic suite with this push: one sensor on the **solar-array normal** (the nominally sun-pointing face) plus additional units on remaining faces for full-sky coverage, and the §8.1 accuracy campaign starts sweeping **sensor-handoff geometry** rather than a single always-in-view boresight.

**Multi-sensor suite and fault-tolerant fusion (Implemented Push 51).** The first half of §8.2 lands: the reference vehicle grows from one sun sensor and one IMU to a **six-face sun-sensor suite and two IMUs**, `AttitudeEstimator` combines rather than merely accepts them, and each sensor type gets the combination rule its redundancy is actually worth. Star-tracker fusion into the MEKF is the next push and is deliberately untouched here.

**The IMU vote, and why averaging is a defect rather than a simplification.** `gnc::ImuVoter` (`lib/gnc/imu_voting.{hpp,cpp}`) implements the committed design above.
