## 24. Suggested Development Phasing (for `CLAUDE.md` build order)

Dependency-ordered so the suite is buildable and testable at every step:

- **Phase 0 — Foundations:** conventions (§3) including **boundary-only typed frame/unit vectors** and the **physical-constants registry**; **canonical state structs** (`EstimatedState`/`TruthState`, §8.0); math/frames/time/ephemeris libraries (fixed-size Eigen, JPL scalar-first quaternions, TAI + GPS/ECEF conversions); config schema + hardware-model library + **config compiler** (§19.3); repo + CI skeleton, **Requirements ICD**, **docs site + GMAT golden-data harness**, license.
- **Phase 1 — Truth sim core:** 6DOF + RK89, gravity (EGM2008, settable order), third-body (SPICE), drag (NRLMSIS 2.1), SRP + eclipse, IGRF-14, disturbance torques. Validate via conservation/analytic + **GMAT golden cases** (propagation/conversions). *Complete (Push 43):* the four §5.3 disturbance-torque providers — gravity-gradient, aero CP–CM, SRP CP–CM and residual dipole — are composed into the plant behind per-torque scenario switches, so rotation now sees the environment and not only actuator torques.
- **Phase 2 — Sensor & actuator models + hardware library:** generic framework + each model with full error stacks; model-ID selection; GNSS reports GPS time/ECEF. *Extended (Push 45):* the generic **payload-sensor** geometry model (§6.3) — any instrument with a boresight, +Z the boresight by convention, conic/square/rectangular fields, the shared §6.1 occlusion, and boresight-to-Sun/nadir angles now reported on every line of sight — plus a quaternion spelling for a unit's mounting in the vehicle config. Radiometry and detector behaviour are per-instrument and deliberately deferred; a flight-side payload data path is not built.
- **Phase 3 — FSW skeleton (F´) + two-process SITL:** topology, rate groups, telemetry/command/event/param, mode-manager stub, **plant↔FSW F´ TCP IPC + sim-time lockstep**, onboard time/EOP/Chebyshev ephemeris, persistence.
- **Phase 4 — Attitude determination:** initializers (TRIAD/QUEST), MEKF fine mode + **coarse mode (SS+MAG+IMU)**, multi-IMU/multi-sun-sensor fusion, validity flags + occlusion handling. *Started (Push 39, 40, 41, 42, 44):* the `lib/gnc` coarse chain — TRIAD with Shuster covariance + the coarse SS+MAG+IMU estimator behind the §10 Safe-mode floor — and the F´ `AttitudeEstimator` component running it on the barrier-driven 10 Hz GNC cycle, fed by the `GncPorts` measurement seam (port arrays, multi-unit ready) and the `OnboardTables`/IGRF-14 references (§8.1). Push 41 closed the config-compiler→`ParameterDb` tuning path the estimator refuses without, so a SITL run with the compiled parameter file now demonstrates closed-loop coarse attitude estimation end to end (§19.3). Push 42 added the fine mode at the `lib/gnc` level — Davenport's q-method N-vector initializer and the 6-state attitude/gyro-bias MEKF with per-update NIS gating and Monte-Carlo-validated NEES/NIS consistency (§8.1). Push 44 wired that filter onto the `AttitudeEstimator` component with real fine↔coarse arbitration — Davenport cold start, demotion on refusal/NIS streaks, the fine coast horizon, filter fault or command, each an FDIR-visible event, over a coarse chain that keeps running underneath so the fallback is never cold — plus seven no-default fine-mode parameters through the same config-compiler path and NIS/bias/covariance telemetry, which **closes REQ-ADET-004**. Remaining: an onboard position source for the magnetic reference that does not depend on a live GNSS fix (§8.3), the multi-unit fusion layer (§8.2), which is also what brings a star tracker into the filter and removes the white-`R` optimism the sun/magnetometer-only budget carries, and the **calibration items** — onboard magnetometer hard/soft-iron calibration and sun-vector albedo correction — that tighten REQ-ADET-005/006 to their committed post-calibration values of 5°/3° (§8.1; the second, non-parallel star tracker that tightens REQ-ADET-007 to 0.02° arrives with §8.2). Push 45 put **numbers** on all of it: the quantitative knowledge requirements REQ-ADET-005/006/007 on the error norm, and REQ-PAY-001 on a payload's cross-boresight error, each set from the Monte Carlo campaign that also guards it in CI (§8.1). Push 46 delivered the first of the two calibration items end to end: the attitude-free hard/soft-iron ellipsoid fit in `lib/gnc/mag_calibration` (stage 1, 0.087° worst-case post-calibration systematic against a 1.93° uncalibrated term) and the commanded flow on the component (stage 2 — `MAG_CAL_START`/`ABORT`/`CLEAR`, live coverage telemetry, one application point ahead of every magnetometer consumer, seven no-default parameters, and a SITL demonstration fitting to 0.14° over a 500 s tumbling window). **Persisting** an applied calibration across a reboot is deferred to §23.6; today it is component state and the ground re-flies the window. Push 47 delivered the second item, the **sun-vector Earth-albedo correction**: the truth model's albedo error became a *directed* pull toward the sunlit Earth rather than a random tilt of the same size (§6.2 — modelling it as noise had made a physically correctable term look uncorrectable), `lib/gnc/albedo_correction` inverts that model in closed form from onboard geometry, and the component applies it at one point ahead of every sun consumer with a **per-cycle choice between a corrected and an uncorrected sun sigma**, because the correction needs a position fix, a sunlit Earth in the field and an attitude to place it with, and a cycle without them must not be weighted as if it had them. With **both** items the campaign projects coarse 3σ 8.49° → 2.77° and fine 8.07° → 2.69°, clearing the committed 5°/3° — the coarse with 45% margin, the fine with 10%, and the whole result turning on the 0.30 albedo dispersion fraction rather than on the correction algorithm, which removes its modelled term exactly (§8.1). REQ-ADET-005/006 nonetheless stayed at 15° through Push 47, the same rule item (1) was held to; Push 48 added the ephemeris grading and Push 50 **enacted** the tightening at 5°/3°, the fine threshold conditioned on the DE440 tables being active. Pushes 51–53 delivered the fusion layer, the star trackers and the mode ladder (§8.2), and Push 65 closed the first item on the list above: position now arrives from the §8.3 `OrbitEstimator` component, so a receiver outage no longer costs the magnetic reference until the orbit filter's own coast horizon.
- **Phase 5 — Attitude control:** B-dot, PID, RW L-norm/L-∞ allocation **or** CMG steering (modular), momentum management + MTQ desaturation; **onboard disturbance feedforward** (§8.5 — model-based gravity-gradient/`m_res×B` first, then the momentum-based residual-torque observer shared with the §9 momentum-anomaly monitor). *Started (Push 54):* the three laws in `lib/gnc` (B-dot with the §7 duty-factor scaling, the quaternion-error PID with clamped and saturation-frozen integration, and the L2/L-∞ wheel allocation) plus the F´ `AttitudeController` that runs them as member 1 of the barrier-driven 10 Hz GNC cycle — and with it the whole **MTQ/MAG duty-cycle interlock** (§7) and its stuck-on rod monitor, which is what let the magnetorquers and the magnetometers finally share a loop. The Phase-4 placeholder `ScriptedCmdSource` was deleted: the rate group's commands are a control law's now. *Push 55* put numbers on what those laws are worth: the `analysis/control/` linear toolkit measures the shipped loop's stability margins on the **sampled-data** loop (60.1°/60.1°/63.1° phase, 25.5/25.5/23.9 dB gain at the Push 60 tuning, REQ-ACTL-006), the actuator suite's controllability including every 3-of-4 wheel-failure subset and the magnetorquers' instantaneous rank deficiency (REQ-ACTL-007), and the estimator's observability against the same information-matrix metric the flight seed gate uses (REQ-ACTL-008) — all from the committed config, and it also fixed the roadmap for the two alternate laws (LQR planned, MPC deferred with a named revisit criterion, §8.5/§18.16). *Push 56* closed the momentum half: `lib/gnc` momentum accounting from the wheel tachometers, the Camillo & Markley cross-product desaturation running **concurrently with pointing** and excluded from detumble, the tier-1/tier-2 **disturbance feedforward** with the tier-2 observer doubling as the §9 momentum-anomaly monitor, and a stored-momentum envelope tied by test to the momentum range Push 55's per-axis margins are valid over (REQ-ACTL-009/-010/-011). *Push 69* added the **per-wheel capacity monitor** the envelope is not: a four-wheel array can hold momentum in its null space (wheels spinning against each other) that sums to nothing in body axes, so the envelope, the desaturation threshold and the observer are all quiet while one wheel walks to its stop; `WheelCapacityNms` (the catalog's `max_momentum_nms`, configc-checked) is watched on the **largest single wheel**, `WheelNearCapacity` fires at 90 % of it, and `MaxWheelMomentumNms` / `NullSpaceMomentumNms` are telemetered — measured on the pre-Push-68 sun-acquisition storm, a single wheel reached 57 % of capacity while the body envelope is 24 % of one wheel. *Push 69* then audited what the storm had flown over — the allocation, the momentum management and the controller's large-signal path — with three independent reviews writing adversarial tests, and built the **wheel-speed bias** (REQ-ACTL-012). What the audit found: the L-∞ allocation minimised the unweighted largest wheel torque although the config carries per-wheel limits (now weighted by each wheel's box; identical on the reference vehicle's equal wheels; verified against a brute-force null-space search to 1e-9); the body-momentum thresholds are **blind to the null space** — the pyramid can spin its wheels against each other at full capacity with zero body momentum, so `MomentumState` now reports the largest wheel and the null-space content and the controller monitors the former against `WheelCapacityNms`; the momentum config accepted coplanar spin axes (refused now); saturation was visible only as edge-gated events, which is exactly how ~80 saturation events per row went unnoticed — every control SITL row now records and bounds its saturation events, envelope events, fine-mode demotions and peak wheel momentum against capacity. Everything else was confirmed against theory and pinned: min-norm exactness, the L-∞ optimum, single-factor scaling with the direction preserved, every 3-of-4 subset, the desaturation law's dissipativity under clamping and duty scaling, the concurrent desat/pointing bookkeeping, and the one negation site between allocation and momentum accounting. **The wheel-speed bias** (`lib/gnc/rw_bias`, `WheelBiasNms`/`WheelBiasGainPerS`/`WheelBiasMaxTorqueNm`) answers the operating point every zero-momentum vehicle drifts to: wheels near zero speed, where the 8e-6 N·m drive LSB, the Coulomb sign flip (a 1.6e-5 N·m step on the body per crossing) and stiction make the wheel a poor actuator. On a redundant array the bias lives in the **null space** of the torque map — on the pyramid `[+b,−b,+b,−b]` — so it spins the wheels and stores no body momentum, costs no desaturation, and its servo torque `k·NNᵀ(h_b − h_w)` has zero body torque for *any* configured pattern (only the null-space projection is pursued; the cap is one scale on the whole vector, never a per-wheel clip, for the same reason); an all-zero pattern is the toggle, and a three-wheel array leaves it inert (`MomentumTargetBody` is the bus-level alternative there). The truth wheel gained the physics the bias is for: **Karnopp stiction** (`stiction_nm`, `stiction_band_rad_s`; the catalog wheel carries 1.6e-5 N·m over 0.5 rad/s) holding a near-rest rotor against a sub-breakaway command so the body sees nothing — which is what "near-zero current does not control the vehicle" means. Measured on the same loaded hold flown both ways: **21 zero crossings → 0**, slowest wheel out of the stiction band, pointing 0.0144° → 0.0119°, no saturation, no capacity approach; and, recorded rather than hidden, the *unloaded* hold points better with the wheels held at rest by stiction (0.0029° vs 0.0102°) because a spinning wheel pays the half of its Coulomb friction the 0.5 feedforward trim leaves — a real cost of the bias on this vehicle and a reason to trim the friction feedforward toward 1 once a rundown is measured, not a reason to fly wheels that do nothing until breakaway. Sizing rule, configc-enforced: bias under `WheelCapacityNms`, and at least 0.75× `MomentumDesatEnterNms` (the pyramid's worst single-wheel share) or a loaded wheel reaches zero before desaturation engages — 10 % of capacity just meets it on this vehicle, and the row's 2 rad/s tail minimum is that margin. *Push 59* built the **wheel-drive friction feedforward** Push 56 recorded as owed (`lib/gnc/rw_friction`, applied per wheel between the allocation and the drive command, with Karnopp's zero-velocity band on the Coulomb sign and a per-wheel trim) and then ablated the drive term by term instead of trusting the attribution: the loaded array goes 2.77° → **1.38°**, friction was about half of Push 56's 2.9°, and the other half is the drive's own 1e-4 N·m torque LSB. Removing friction from the budget also made the Push 56 disturbance-feedforward comparison decisive for the first time (18 % against the old 2 %). Push 59 also built the **tier-3 residual-dipole estimator** (`lib/gnc/dipole_estimation`, §8.5) and measured that it cannot refine *this* vehicle's feedforward — the reference dipole's signature is ~40× under the tier-2 observer's noise floor, and precision saturates at the forgetting horizon rather than the pass length, so no pass of any length reaches it — which is why it ships as a tested library with its component wiring deliberately deferred to a vehicle or an investigation in the dipole class its gates say is resolvable. *Push 60* then found that the vehicle those measurements were taken on was flying the wrong wheel: `analysis/sizing` (§12) measured the RW-X pyramid at **78×** the largest momentum any sizing driver asks of a 12 kg bus, growing the bus to suit it kills magnetic detumble outright (the saturated-gain convergence bound, §8.5), and the repair was the generic wheel entry re-sized in place to 30 mN·m·s plus a loop retuned around it — `ω_n` 0.197 → 0.8 rad/s, the momentum envelope 2.0e-3 → 7.2e-3 N·m·s, `DetumbleExitRadps` 0.5 → 1.9 deg/s. What that bought, measured on the same rows: inertial hold 0.41° → **0.0094°**, the loaded array 1.38° → **0.0396°** (so the drive-quantization item above is closed by an 8e-6 N·m LSB rather than by dither), and a detumble exit threshold that finally clears both the momentum bound and B-dot's own 1.71 deg/s sensing floor, which were mutually unsatisfiable before. Still owed in this phase: **CMG steering**, the tier-3 **SRP scale factor** (the drag half shipped with Push 76, §8.3; the SRP half needs an onboard SRP term to scale) and the dipole estimator's component seam, the friction blend deadband's lower bound (no catalog wheel carries a tachometer specification), and the orbital-timescale detumble campaign REQ-ACTL-001 names. *Push 68* closed the **POINT-from-tumble entry** Push 67 measured: from a 3 deg/s handover the wheels sat pinned, stored momentum left the envelope and desaturation cycled, and the SUN_MAG fine mode was demoted `NIS_STREAK` a dozen times before it held — because `K_p·δθ` at a 100° error is ~85× the torque limit, so the PID was a bang-bang slew with only the torque clip between it and the wheels. The fix is Wie & Lu's rate-limited eigenaxis form [wie1995] inside the same PID: the proportional term is a commanded rate `(K_p/K_d)·δθ` saturated **by norm** at `PidMaxSlewRateRadps` (0.5 deg/s on the reference vehicle, sized so `K_d·ω_max` is 74 % of the torque budget), so outside the limit POINT is a constant-rate eigenaxis slew with pure rate damping and inside it the unchanged PID; a limited cycle freezes the integrator like a saturated one. Measured on the same row: fine-mode demotions 13 → **1**, torque-saturation events ~80 → **2**, no momentum excursion, tail 0.012°, and both are now asserted (≤ 2 demotions, ≤ 10 saturations). The `-c` bench hook and the DETUMBLE→POINT handover need no rate gate now — the entry itself is safe. *Push 61* closed the **saturated-gain magnetorquer criterion** the tool was missing (M4, §8.5).
- **Phase 6 — Orbit determination & propagation:** GNSS-sim, onboard MEKF OD + self-covariance, multi-object propagation, batch LS, SGP4, CCSDS OEM (GMAT-validated). *Started:* the `lib/gnc` orbit filter — a 6-state EKF on ECI position/velocity over a coarse **8×8 EGM2008** + exponential-drag force model, with the GPS→TAI / ECEF→ECI ingest conversion, per-fix anisotropic `R` from the receiver's own reported accuracy, χ²₃ NIS gating, a **fix-latency correction** applied to the measurement rather than the filter, and a 300 s coast horizon past which the solution is dropped so the next fix re-acquires whole (§8.3). Verified the two ways that are not interchangeable: GMAT at **matched fidelity** (3.5 mm two-body, 1.03 cm J2 — which found two geopotential constant-pairing defects on its first run — and 1.2 cm at 8×8 against GMAT's EGM96, which independently validates the flight field's recursion, de-normalised table and ECEF handling) and a NEES/NIS Monte Carlo for covariance consistency (6.03 against 6, 3.01 against 3). Its **process noise is sized from a measurement**, not tuned: the model's divergence from the GMAT-validated truth sim at 32×32 over the coast horizon is **1.28 m**, giving **`q_a = 1.8e-7 m²/s³`**, and the measurement is a CI-asserted bound. The 8×8 field replaced a closed-form J2 model that diverged 3.17 m over the same arc, and both models are flown every run so the improvement is a live gate — the *reference* had to be raised from 8×8 to 32×32 at the same time, because a truncation measured against a reference the model now matches reports a small number and asserts nothing. Push 65 wired it: the `OrbitEstimator` component runs first in the GNC rate group and serves its solution to the attitude estimator (which no longer sees the receiver), the SITL fault matrix carries the GNSS-outage rows, and configc holds `MaxFixLatencyS` against the receiver's catalogued latency. Push 66 flew the rest of the §9.2 GNSS rows on the topology — spoof step inside and past the horizon, a stale receiver clock refused by name, `OD_RESET` mid-run, a second receiver carrying a primary outage (the topology now wires two), and one full orbit period holding one solution — in their own file, `sitl_od_fault_test.cpp`. Push 70 gave the filter what a review of Ceresoli et al. (2025) said it lacked: a **non-gravitational acceleration input** fed by the §17 burn executor's commanded thrust (26 m vs 1374 m at the end of a 900 s outage with a burn inside it), a **degraded coast horizon** (FINE 300 s → DEGRADED 1800 s, the reference kept, a 20-min coast at 2.2 m), a **ground seed** command, and receiver latency jitter in the sim. Push 71 applied the NESC navigation-filter best practices (NASA/TP-2018-219822 Ch. 9; TB 20-03 items d–g) to both filters: re-tune on upload without losing the solution, covariance re-initialisation on command, an accept/inhibit/force policy per measurement type, a propagated backup ephemeris with restart and divergence comparator, and a per-cycle covariance definiteness check; Push 72 took the attitude filter through the TP's Ch. 3/5/8 items (order-invariant update, Reynolds reset, Gauss-Markov bias option, star-tracker latency modelled and compensated); Push 73 built the orbit filter's Ch. 2 items (RTN SNC, DMC acceleration states, σ_SMA/FPA metrics) and measured that on this vehicle the DMC layer worsens the coast while an RTN restructuring improved it on three runs; Push 74 put that candidate through the 30-run gate, where the improvement proved to be sampling noise — the flown tuning stays, every reduced-`q_a` variant is refused by the NEES with numbers, and the gate run turned up a defect in the campaign's own reading (a clean-fix rejection *rate* that was counting a post-blind-burn re-acquisition as gate false alarms), now split into a quality-scoped false-alarm rate and a re-acquisition bound of its own (REQ-ODP-012). Remaining: the raw pseudorange path and the receiver-clock states that only become observable with it, a 3-state DMC augmentation, a re-derived coast horizon now that the truncation is 2.5× smaller, and everything else in the bullet list above.
- **Phase 7 — Guidance + full state machine:** pointing modes, slew planning with keep-out/keep-in cones, GS tracking, complete mode set. **The state machine is built the §10.1 way** and that is a phase item in its own right, not an implementation detail of the mode set: the satellite-wide modes and GNC submodes are **defined declaratively in `config/`** (states, hierarchical scoping, transitions, guards over a bounded telemetry-predicate vocabulary, entry/exit actions from a closed dispatch set), **compiled by `configc` into flight tables** validated for reachability and Safe-sink coverage, and executed by **one generic bounded table interpreter** — no code generation of transition logic. The same source emits the **auto-generated state diagram** (Mermaid/DOT) rendered into the Sphinx site, so the machine is reviewed as a diagram and a change to it is a diff to one file. F´ v4.2.2's native FPP state machines were evaluated and are the documented fallback (§10.1.3); they stay the right tool for machines internal to a single component. The `AttitudeEstimator`'s hand-coded ladder is not rewritten — it becomes the first instance the architecture describes (§10.1.5).
- **Phase 8 — Maneuvering + interop outputs:** thruster targeting (SMA/altitude) with **finite burns as the default for every burn type** (impulsive = first guess only, §17), Delta-V mode, pre/post-burn CCSDS/TLE, STK/FreeFlyer export.
- **Phase 9 — Subsystems:** simple power, thermal, comms/link budget; subsystem monitors into FDIR; **CCSDS CFDP (Class 1/2) file transfer** over the `ComCcsds` stack (§20 — adopt upstream F´ CFDP if available by then, else implement; decision checkpoint at the §22.4 push).
- **Phase 10 — FDIR:** monitors, isolation, responses, safing escalation across sensors/actuators/subsystems; **fault-injection integration suite** (sensor faults/loss, occlusions, GPS outage/spoofing, actuator faults, subsystem limits, cascades — §23.1.1); **reboot-surviving time-tagged sequencing** (§23.6 — persisted absolute/relative-time sequences that resume past-due-skipped after a reset).
- **Phase 11 — Monte Carlo + analysis tools:** dispersion framework, momentum/sizing, detumble MC, contact scheduling, link budget, consistency metrics, per-REQ margin reporting; **RW jitter waterfall tool** (spin sweep → PSD-per-speed-bin spectrograms from the §7 harmonic model, keep-out-zone extraction feeding the §8.5 table); the **ground-solved LQR gain** for the §8.5 alternate control law. *Started early (Push 55):* the `analysis/control/` linear toolkit — stability margins, controllability and observability of the as-flown configuration — landed alongside the Phase-5 control laws rather than waiting, because the margin requirement REQ-ACTL-006 is about the gains Push 54 committed and a margin measured a phase later is a margin nobody checked. *Push 59* added `analysis/detumble/`, the B-dot residual-spin-tail campaign. *Push 60* added `analysis/sizing/` — the momentum/sizing item on this line — for the same reason the control toolkit came early: it validates the actuator suite and the tuning the earlier phases committed, and it immediately found four things nothing else was checking (§12). It also exposes a **requirement gap**: nothing in the baseline is written on actuator sizing, so its criteria deliberately carry no requirement IDs and the report says so.
- **Post-Phase-11 / with the trigger that needs it — MIMO control analysis (committed, §8.5).** Multivariable loop shaping, disk margins with all channels perturbed simultaneously at the plant input and output, and structured-singular-value robustness where warranted — required the moment the plant stops being near-diagonal: CMGs, flexible appendages, momentum-biased operation, or an LQR/MPC full-matrix gain. Scheduled alongside the CMG and flexible-appendage work rather than as a standalone push, because it is the analysis those items cannot ship without. The implementation choice (extend the native scipy modules vs adopt `python-control` + `slycot` for μ-analysis) is deferred to that push.
- **Phase 12 — Advanced / future:** CMG singularity refinements, main propulsion (chemical: Isp/thrust/mass-flow/min-impulse; electric: low-thrust/throttle-curve/EPS-coupled — §5.4/§7), fuel slosh (per-tank pendulum / spring-mass-damper, §5.4), **hinged solar arrays** (lumped panel-on-torsional-hinge model: CM/inertia contribution, first-mode frequency vs control bandwidth, hinge reaction torques — §5.4), CG-migration/inertia depletion coupling, the remaining half of onboard **disturbance-parameter estimation** — the **SRP** scale factor, which needs an onboard SRP term to scale (the residual-dipole half shipped with Phase 5 and the **drag** scale factor with Push 76, §8.5 tier 3 / §8.3) — RPOD module fill-in, **live web tools** (§21.4).

Cross-cutting throughout: unit-to-integration tests, static analysis, sourced documentation, and traceability.

---

*End of design baseline. §18 decisions resolved; the §23.7 pre-kickoff checklist is
closed and `CLAUDE.md` is generated. This document stays the authoritative spec —
per-section "Implemented (Push N)" notes record where the build has reached; the rollup
lives in `PROGRESS.md`.*
